\ref{Qf-monic}\Leftrightarrow\ref{f-infl}\Leftrightarrow\ref{h-1-factors}

are dual; and the last assertion is an immediate consequence.
∎

6.2.
A localization sequence in
\mathsf{wWald}
induced by
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}

Although (
6.5
)
is an exact sequence in
\mathsf{Extri}
,
the sequences

\mathscr{N}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}^{R}\overset{Q}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}
and
\mathscr{N}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}^{L}\overset{Q}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}

need not be.
They do, however, induce localization sequences in
\mathsf{wWald}
. We will use Theorem
3.6
to show this in Theorem
6.14
, and so
we setup some notation to help the exposition.

Notation 6.12
.

We let
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
(resp.
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
) denote the canonical wbiWET category corresponding to the extriangulated category
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
(resp.
\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}
); see Example
5.4
. That is,
\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
is the collection of all
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-conflations,

\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}_{\mathscr{N}}
is the collection of all
\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}
-conflations,
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Iso}}\mathscr{C}
.
Accordingly, we denote by
\operatorname{\mathsf{C}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathsf{F}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
) the cofibrations (resp. fibrations) in
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
.

By Theorem
6.10
(2)
, we know that
\mathscr{N}
is a thick subcategory of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}
, so we associate classes of morphisms
\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\coloneqq\mathcal{R}
as in Definition
4.13
to
\mathscr{N}
:

(i)

\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}=\Set{f\in\mathscr{C}^{\to}}{f\text{ is an }\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}\text{-inflation with }\operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}(f)\in\mathscr{N}}
;

(ii)

\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}}=\Set{g\in\mathscr{C}^{\to}}{g\text{ is an }\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}\text{-deflation with }\operatorname{\mathsf{CoCone}}(g)\in\mathscr{N}}
; and

(iii)

(\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}})_{\mathsf{ret}}=\Set{g\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}}}{g\textnormal{ is a retraction}}
.

Put
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}\coloneqq\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
, the smallest subclass of morphisms closed under compositions containing both
\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
. Moreover, by Theorem
6.10
(2)
, we have

(6.6)

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=(\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}})_{\mathsf{ret}}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}.

Since
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
is weak Waldhausen, we can consider the subcategory

(\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})
of
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
-acyclic objects (see Lemma
2.20
). In fact, in the proof of Theorem
6.14
we will see that Setup
3.1
is satisfied, and hence we can consider the classes of morphisms given in Definition
3.2
:

(iv)

(\mathcal{L}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}\coloneqq\operatorname{\mathsf{C}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\cap\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
;

(v)

(\mathcal{R}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}\coloneqq\operatorname{\mathsf{F}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\cap\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
; and

(vi)

\mathcal{T}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\coloneqq\set{g\in(\mathcal{R}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}}{\text{there is an }\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\text{-conflation }A\overset{f}{\rightarrowtail}B\overset{g}{\twoheadrightarrow}C\text{ with }A\in\mathscr{N}}
.

We use the extra sub/superscripts above only to emphasise the origin of these classes of morphisms.

Comparing some of the classes of morphisms above, we observe the following.

Lemma 6.13
.

We have

(\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}})_{\mathsf{ret}}\subseteq\mathcal{T}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}

and

\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}\subseteq(\mathcal{L}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}
.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the fact that
Q
sends an
\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}
-conflation to a short exact sequence and that
\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
is saturated.
For the second claim, suppose
f\colon A\to B
belongs to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}
. We only need to show that
f\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.
There is a triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
\mathscr{C}
,
where
h[-1]
(and
h
) factor through
\mathscr{N}
(see Proposition
6.3
),
and with
C\in\mathscr{N}
.
Moreover,
C\in\mathscr{N}
implies
g
factors through
\mathscr{N}
and hence
f\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
by Lemma
6.11
, so
f\in(\mathcal{L}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}
and we are done.
∎

In the following main result of this subsection,
the weak Waldhausen category
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})

can be thought of as a “one-sided” localization of
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}=(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})
.

Theorem 6.14
.

The triplet
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
is a wbiWET category and
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
is a wWET category both with respect to
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
, and
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
.
Moreover, there is a localization sequence

(6.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{inc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}}}

of wWET categories, which induces the following exact sequence of abelian groups.

(6.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})}

Corresponding statements also hold for
\mathscr{C}^{L}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
is an extriangulated category, the triplet
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
is just the canonical wbiWET category with respect to
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
by Example
5.4
.
Now we shall show that
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
is weak Waldhausen, from which it will follow that it is wWET with respect to
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
Conditions
(1)(WC0)
–
(1)(WC2)
are satisfied as they already hold for
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
, and

(1)(WW0)
follows from
(M0)
for
\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.
To show the Gluing Axiom
(1)(WW1)
, consider the diagram (
2.3
), where
x,y,z
all lie in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
5.8
, we obtain a morphism (
5.1
) of
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-triangles in
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
Since
Q
is right exact on
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-triangles by Theorem
6.10
(5)
, the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QA_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QB_{1}\oplus QC_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QD_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QA_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QB_{2}\oplus QC_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QD_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
-c_{1}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Qx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\begin{pmatrix}b_{1},\amph g_{1}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(y\oplus z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Qw}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\begin{pmatrix}f_{2}\\
-c_{2}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\begin{pmatrix}b_{2},\amph g_{2}\end{pmatrix}}

of right exact sequences in
\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}

shows that
Qw
is an isomorphism.
Thus,
w\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
as
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
is saturated.

Thus, we have established that (
6.7
) is a sequence of skeletally small weak Waldhausen categories and, moreover, Setup
3.1
is met.
We wish to use Theorem
3.6
to deduce (
6.7
) is a localization sequence in
\mathsf{wWald}
, so we must show that

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
consists of finite compositions of morphisms from

(\mathcal{L}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\mathsf{ac}}\cup\mathcal{T}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\cup\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}
(see Notation
6.12
(iv)
,
(vi)
).
We have

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=(\mathcal{R}_{\mathscr{N}})_{\mathsf{ret}}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{N}}
(see (
6.6
))
and Lemma
6.13
tells us this is enough.
The localization sequence (
6.7
) induces the following right exact sequence in
\mathsf{Ab}
.

(6.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}})}

Recall that
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
denotes the wbiWET category corresponding to
\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
As
\mathscr{N}
is thick in
\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{N}}
by Theorem
6.10
(2)
, we can apply Lemma
5.12
to obtain
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
and that
(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})

is the weak biWaldhausen category corresponding to the extriangulated category

(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})
.
It follows from Remark
6.8
(2)
that

(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})=(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})

is the wbiWET category corresponding to the extriangulated category

(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{N}}^{R}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}^{R}|_{\mathscr{N}})=(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})
.
In particular, we have
K_{0}(\mathscr{N},(\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})=K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})
(see Example
5.1
).

We also have

K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})=K_{0}(\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})
by Example
5.1
again, and so (
6.9
) is

(6.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}})}

To see that (
6.8
) is exact, it is enough to show

K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})
is an isomorphism.
The assumption
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}(\mathscr{N},\mathscr{N})=\mathscr{C}
, which is equivalent to
\operatorname{\mathsf{CoCone}}(\mathscr{N},\mathscr{N})=\mathscr{C}
, implies that each object
C\in\mathscr{C}
admits a triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C[1]}

in
\mathscr{C}
with
N_{i}\in\mathscr{N}
.
By Proposition
6.3
,
this triangle yields the
\mathfrak{s}_{\mathscr{N}}^{R}
-conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1},}

which is thus a finite
\mathscr{N}
-coresolution of
C\in\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
(see
[
OS23
, Def. 4.2]
).
Second, we have that
\mathscr{N}
is extension-closed in
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
, and it closed under taking cones of
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-inflations by Theorem
6.10
(1)
.
Hence, the dual of
[
OS23
, Thm 4.5]
shows that
K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})
is an isomorphism with inverse given by
[C]\mapsto[N_{0}]-[N_{1}]
.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

Unlike the case of the extriangulated localization, such as Proposition
5.11
,
the wWET category
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
appearing in Theorem
6.14
is not wcoWET with respect to
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
in general.
Therefore, (
6.7
) is not necessarily a sequence of wbiWET categories.

6.3.
The JS index isomorphism via localization

Our aim in this last part of Section
6
is to prove Corollary
6.6
via some form of reduction technique. Reduction techniques concerning
n
-cluster tilting subcategories have appeared several times before, e.g.
[
IY08
,
AI12
,
Jas15
,
FMP23
]
.
In the rest of this section,
\mathscr{X}
denotes an
n
-cluster tilting subcategory of the triangulated category
\mathscr{C}
(see Definition
6.1
).
We also put
\mathscr{N}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}^{\perp_{0}}
, so that we are in a special case of Setup
6.7
.

For each integer
1\leq i\leq n-1
, we put
\mathscr{N}_{i}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}[i]*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[n-1]
.
There is a cotorsion pair

(\mathscr{X}*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[i-1],\mathscr{N}_{i}[-1])
on
\mathscr{C}
in the sense of
[
Nak11
, Def. 2.1]
by
[
Bel15
, Prop. 3.2, Thm. 3.4]
, and so
\mathscr{N}_{i}
is extension-closed and closed under direct summands in the triangulated category
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.
Note that
\mathscr{N}_{n-1}=\mathscr{X}[n-1]
, there are inclusions

\mathscr{N}_{n-1}\subseteq\mathscr{N}_{n-2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathscr{N}_{1}\subseteq\mathscr{C}
, and

\mathscr{N}_{1}=\mathscr{X}^{\perp_{0}}=\mathscr{N}
.
Recall that an object
P
in an extriangulated category
(\mathscr{D},\mathbb{F},\mathfrak{t})
is
\mathbb{F}
-projective
if
\mathbb{F}(P,D)=0
for all
D\in\mathscr{D}
; see
[
NP19
, Def. 3.23, Prop. 3.24]
.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
i\in\set{1,\ldots,n-1}
.
The following assertions hold.

(1)

The subcategory
\mathscr{N}_{i}\subseteq\mathscr{C}
inherits extriangulated structures
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
from the triangulated category
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
and
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
from the extriangulated category
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

(2)

We have

(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})=(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
.

(3)

The subcategory of
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projectives in
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
coincides with
\mathscr{X}[i]
and
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
has enough
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projectives.
In particular,
\mathscr{X}[i]
is contravariantly finite, rigid and closed under direct summands in
\mathscr{N}_{i}
.

Proof.

(1)
follows immediately from
\mathscr{N}_{i}
being extension-closed in
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
and hence also in
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

(2)
is a consequence of

(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})=(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}})

(see Remark
6.8
(2)
)
and
\mathscr{N}_{i}\subseteq\mathscr{N}
.

For
(3)
, we see that
\mathscr{X}[i]
consists of
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projective objects as
(\mathscr{X}*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[i-1],\mathscr{N}_{i}[-1])
is a cotorsion pair, hence

\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}(\mathscr{X}[i],\mathscr{N}_{i})=\mathbb{E}(\mathscr{X}[i],\mathscr{N}_{i})=0
.
If
i=n-1
, then there is nothing to show, so suppose
i\leq n-2
.
Since
\mathscr{N}_{i}=\mathscr{X}[i]*(\mathscr{X}[i+1]*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[n-1])
, we have

\mathscr{N}_{i}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}(\mathscr{X}[i]*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[n-2],\mathscr{X}[i])
, which shows that
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
has enough
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projectives.
If
C
is
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projective, then this also shows that it must be a direct summand of an object in
\mathscr{X}[i]
and so
C\in\mathscr{X}[i]
. Thus,
\mathscr{X}[i]
is precisely the collection of
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projectives.
The remaining claims follow from this.
∎

To simplify notation, we put
K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i})\coloneqq K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
.
Note that the extriangulated structure

(\mathscr{N}_{n-1},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{n-1}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{n-1}})

is just the split exact structure on
\mathscr{X}[n-1]
since
\mathbb{E}(\mathscr{X}[n-2],\mathscr{X}[n-2])\cong\mathbb{E}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{X})=0
, and so
K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{n-1})=K^{\mathsf{sp}}_{0}(\mathscr{X}[n-1])
.

For any additive category
\mathscr{A}
, we denote the category of finitely presented functors from
\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{op}}
to
\mathsf{Ab}
by
\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{A}
. Recall that a functor
\mathsf{M}\colon\mathscr{A}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{Ab}
is
finitely presented
if there is an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}(-,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

for some objects
A,B\in\mathscr{A}
.
For each
0\leq i\leq n-1
, consider the restricted Yoneda embedding

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}\colon\mathscr{N}_{i}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[i])
that sends an object
C\in\mathscr{N}_{i}
to the contravariant additive functor

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(C)\coloneqq\mathscr{N}_{i}(-,C)|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}
.
Note that

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}
is essentially surjective and

\operatorname{Ker}(\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]})=\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
(viewed as subcategories of
\mathscr{N}_{i}
) for
1\leq i\leq n-2
.
Furthermore, since the functor
C\mapsto\mathscr{C}(-,C)
is a cohomological functor
\mathbb{Y}\colon\mathscr{C}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{C}
, we know that
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}
sends
an
\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-conflation
A\to B\to C
to an exact sequence

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(A)\to\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(B)\to\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(C)
in
\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[i])
.
The next result says this is actually a right exact sequence.

Lemma 6.17
.

For
1\leq i\leq n-1
, the functor

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}\colon(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[i])

is right exact in the sense of Definition
6.9
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{X}[i]
is the subcategory of
\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-projectives in
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
by Lemma
6.16
(3)
, we have

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(A[1])=\mathscr{N}_{i}(\mathscr{X}[i],A[1])=\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}(\mathscr{X}[i],A)=0

for any
A\in\mathscr{N}_{i}
.
The result follows.
∎

Although the functor
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}}\colon\mathscr{C}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{X}
fails to be right exact in general, its restriction to
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
is so.
Indeed, by
[
OS23
, Exam. 3.20(2)]
we know that there is an exact equivalence

G\colon\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{X}
of abelian categories with
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}}=GQ
. Thus, as
Q\colon\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\to\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}
is right exact (see Theorem
6.10
(5)
), we have that

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}}\colon\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{X}
is right exact.

In particular, we have the diagram

(6.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[n-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[n-2]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}}}

in which the horizontal arrows are the canonical inclusions and the
vertical arrows are the right exact restricted Yoneda functors.
To establish the JS index isomorphism (
6.4
), we will show there is a chain of isomorphisms as follows, induced by the top row of (
6.11
).

(6.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathsf{sp}}_{0}(\mathscr{X}[n-1])=K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{n-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{n-2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Indeed, the JS index isomorphism then follows from noting that the equivalence
\mathscr{X}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{X}[n-1]
yields an isomorphism

\zeta\colon K^{\mathsf{sp}}_{0}(\mathscr{X})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K^{\mathsf{sp}}_{0}(\mathscr{X}[n-1])
given by

(6.13)

[X]\overset{\zeta}{\longmapsto}(-1)^{n-1}[X[n-1]].

The sign is chosen because of the equality

[X]=(-1)^{n-1}[X[n-1]]
in
K_{0}(\mathscr{C})=K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
, the Grothendieck group of the triangulated category
\mathscr{C}
.

As a first step in the proof of Corollary
6.6
, we already have the rightmost isomorphism in (
6.12
) by Theorem
6.14
.
The remaining steps of our reduction process from
\mathscr{N}_{i}
to
\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
will be shown by mimicking the proof of Theorem
6.14
.
To this end, we will impose natural wWET structures on
\mathscr{N}_{i}
for any
1\leq i\leq n-1
. We put (cf. Notation
6.12
):

(i)

\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
to be the collection of all
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-conflations;

(ii)

\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i}\coloneqq\operatorname{\mathsf{Iso}}\mathscr{N}_{i}
; and

(iii)

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}\coloneqq\Set{f\in\mathscr{N}^{\to}_{i}}{\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(f)\text{ is an isomorphism}}
.

It follows that
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i})
is the canonical wbiWET category associated to
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
.
We remark that making the analogous declarations for
i=0
would yield the wWET categories
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
and
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
, using the equivalence
G\colon\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}\mathscr{X}
to see
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{0}=\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

Lemma 6.18
.

The triplet

(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i})

is a wWET category with respect to
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}})
.

Proof.

Since
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i})
is a weak Waldhausen category,
we need only show
(1)(WW0)
and
(1)(WW1)
.
Since
(1)(WW0)
is clear,
suppose we are in the setup (
2.3
) of the Gluing Axiom
(1)(WW1)
.
Then we obtain a morphism
(
5.1
)
of
\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-conflations.
We apply the right exact functor

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}

to (
5.1
).
Since
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(x)
and

\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(y\oplus z)

are isomorphisms in
\operatorname{\mathsf{mod}}(\mathscr{X}[i])
,
we have that
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(w)
must also be an isomorphism
using the right exactness of
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}
(see Lemma
6.17
), so
w\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}
.
∎

The last preparatory lemma we need is the following.

Lemma 6.19
.

For each
1\leq i\leq n-2
, there is a sequence of wWET categories as follows

(6.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{i+1},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i+1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{inc}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}}

where

(\mathscr{N}_{i+1},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i+1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i+1})
is the subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}
-acyclic objects in
(\mathscr{N}_{i},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{i})
.
Moreover, the induced homomorphism

K_{0}(\mathsf{inc}_{i})\colon K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i+1})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We know all the triplets in (
6.14
)
are wWET by Lemma
6.18
.
The equality
\mathscr{N}_{i}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}}=\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
follows from
\operatorname{Ker}(\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]})=\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
and the definition of
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{i}
.

The last assertion will follow from the dual of
[
OS23
, Thm. 4.5]
once we note the following.
First, any object in
\mathscr{N}_{i}
has a finite
\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
-coresolution because
\mathscr{N}_{i}=\operatorname{\mathsf{CoCone}}(\mathscr{N}_{i+1},\mathscr{X}[i+1])
and
\mathscr{X}[i+1]\subseteq\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
.
Second, suppose
A\to B\to C
is an
\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}_{i}}
-conflation with
A,B\in\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
. Using Lemma
6.17
and
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(B)=0
, we have
\mathbb{Y}|_{\mathscr{X}[i]}(C)=0
and so
C\in\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
.
∎

Due to Lemma
6.19
, we have confirmed that
(
6.11
) induces the following diagram of wWET categories.

(6.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{n-1},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{n-1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{n-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{1},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{0}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{1},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N}_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}|_{\mathscr{N}_{0}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}_{0})}

In (
6.11
)
each segment of shape
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

does not necessarily belong to
\mathsf{Extri}
,
whereas the entirety of (
6.15
) sits in
\mathsf{wWald}
.
We can now prove the JS index isomorphism (
6.4
).

Proof of Corollary
6.6
.

Since we have obtained the isomorphisms in (
6.12
), we have established that the inclusion
\mathscr{X}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
induces an isomorphism

K_{0}^{\mathsf{sp}}(\mathscr{X})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}})
. We need only show that its inverse is as described in Corollary
6.6
.

Let
\mathscr{N}_{0}\coloneqq\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
, so that we have an isomorphism

K_{0}(\mathsf{inc}_{i})\colon K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i+1})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i})
induced by the inclusion

\mathsf{inc}_{i}\colon\mathscr{N}_{i+1}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{i}

for each
i\in\set{0,\ldots,n-2}
; see Theorem
6.14
and Lemma
6.19
.
Let
\eta_{i}\colon K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i+1})
denote the inverse of
K_{0}(\mathsf{inc}_{i})
.
Each isomorphism
\eta_{i}
is given by coresolving a given object via the equality
\mathscr{N}_{i}=\operatorname{\mathsf{CoCone}}(\mathscr{N}_{i+1},\mathscr{X}[i+1])
, namely, for any generator
[N_{i}]\in K_{0}(\mathscr{N}_{i})
, we have

\eta_{i}([N_{i}])=[N_{i+1}]-[X_{i}[i+1]]

where

N_{i}\to N_{i+1}\to X_{i}[i+1]

is an
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-conflation
in
\mathscr{N}_{i}
with
N_{i+1}\in\mathscr{N}_{i+1}
and
X_{i}\in\mathscr{X}
.
Note that if
X\in\mathscr{X}
, then for all
0\leq i\leq n-2
we have an
\mathfrak{s}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
-triangle of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X[i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X[i+1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{X[i+1]}}

in
\mathscr{N}_{i}
, and hence

\eta_{i}[X[i]]=-[X[i+1]]
.
Thus, for an arbitrary object
N_{0}\coloneqq C\in\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}
,
we have:

\displaystyle[C]=[N_{0}]

\displaystyle\overset{\eta_{0}}{\longmapsto}[N_{1}]-[X_{0}[1]]

\displaystyle\overset{\eta_{1}}{\longmapsto}[N_{2}]-[X_{1}[2]]+[X_{0}[2]]

\displaystyle\overset{\eta_{2}}{\longmapsto}[N_{3}]-[X_{2}[3]]+[X_{1}[3]]-[X_{0}[3]]

\displaystyle{}\hskip 10.0pt\vdots

\displaystyle\overset{\eta_{n-2}}{\longmapsto}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-1-i}[X_{i}[n-1]]

\displaystyle\overset{\zeta}{\longmapsto}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{i}[X_{i}]=\operatorname{\mathsf{ind}}_{\mathscr{X}}(C)

\displaystyle(\text{see }\eqref{eqn:iso-X-Xn-1})

This is indeed the same as the JS index isomorphism (
6.4
), because the above coresolving procedure yields the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{n-3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-1}[-(n-1)]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-2}[-(n-2)]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}

and hence a finite
\mathscr{X}
-resolution of
C
. This is an
\mathscr{X}
-resolution in
\mathscr{C}^{R}_{\mathscr{N}}

as each triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{i+1}[-(i+1)]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{i}[-i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{i+1}[-i]}

satisfies
N_{i+1}[-i]\in\mathscr{X}[1]*\cdots*\mathscr{X}[n-1-i]\subseteq\mathscr{X}^{\perp_{0}}=\mathscr{N}
.
∎

7.
Sarazola’s
K
-theory localization

In
[
Sar20
]
, Sarazola showed that there exists a natural Waldhausen structure on an exact category arising from a single cotorsion pair as an attempt to generalize Hovey’s abelian model structure corresponding to a certain twin cotorsion pair, see
[
Hov02
, Thm. 2.2]
and
[
NP19
, Sec. 4]
.
A localization theorem of
K
-theory is also established there (see
[
Sar20
, Thm. 3]
).
In this section we will investigate Sarazola’s construction in the extriangulated setting.

7.1.
Weak Waldhausen categories following Sarazola

Motivated by
[
Yu22
, Sec. 3]
, we will work under Setup
7.1
in this subsection.
An
\mathbb{E}
-injective
object in an extriangulated category
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is defined dually to an
\mathbb{E}
-projective. Moreover,
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})

has enough
\mathbb{E}
-injectives
if each object
A\in\mathscr{C}
admits an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

A\longrightarrow I_{A}\longrightarrow\Sigma A
with
I_{A}
an
\mathbb{E}
-injective.
In this case,
\Sigma A
is called a
cosyzygy
of
A
.

Setup 7.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be a skeletally small, weakly idempotent complete, extriangulated category with enough
\mathbb{E}
-injectives.
We denote by
\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\operatorname{\mathsf{-Inj}}(\mathscr{C})\subseteq\mathscr{C}
the full subcategory of
\mathbb{E}
-injective objects.
In addition, we fix a full subcategory
\mathscr{N}\subseteq\mathscr{C}
containing
\mathcal{I}
that is extension-closed, and is closed under direct summands and cones of
\mathfrak{s}
-inflations.

Although
\mathscr{N}
is not a thick subcategory of
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
in general, following Definition
4.13
we consider classes of morphisms:

•

\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\coloneqq\Set{f\in\mathscr{C}^{\to}}{f\ \textnormal{is an $\mathfrak{s}$-inflation with}\ \operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}(f)\in\mathscr{N}}
;

•

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\Set{g\in\mathscr{C}^{\to}}{g\textnormal{\ is an $\mathfrak{s}$-deflation with }\operatorname{\mathsf{CoCone}}(g)\in\mathcal{I}}
; and

•

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

Note that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
is the class of retractions in
\mathscr{C}
with the kernels sitting in
\mathcal{I}
, thanks to the weakly idempotent completeness of
\mathscr{C}
(see
[
BHST22
, Prop. 2.5]
) and the
\mathbb{E}
-injectivity of objects in
\mathcal{I}
.

In the sequel, we will construct the following localization sequence of weak Waldhausen categories, which is an analogue of Sarazola’s construction
[
Sar20
, Thm. 6.1]
.
Indeed, if we assume
\mathscr{C}
is an exact category closed under kernels of epimorphisms and
\mathscr{N}
is thick in addition to Setup
7.1
, then we have the setup of
[
Sar20
, Thm. 6.1]
.
Denote by
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
the canonical wbiWET category corresponding to the extriangulated category
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
(see Example
5.4
).

Proposition 7.2
.

There is a sequence

(7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{inc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}}}

in
\mathsf{wWald}
,
where
\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}=\mathscr{N}
and which induces the following exact sequence in
\mathsf{Ab}
.

(7.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{inc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}})}

Before proving the above, we show that an example of Setup
7.1
arises via hereditary cotorsion pairs of extriangulated categories (see
[
Gil16
]
for the exact case).

Example 7.3
.

For this example, we assume that
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
also has enough
\mathbb{E}
-projectives, which is defined dually to having enough
\mathbb{E}
-injectives.
A
syzygy

\Omega A
of
A\in\mathscr{C}
is defined dually to a cosyzygy.
Then one can define the
higher extensions

\mathbb{E}^{i}(-,-)
of the bifunctor
\mathbb{E}(-,-)
, namely, one puts
\mathbb{E}^{i}(C,A)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}(C,\Sigma^{i+1}A)\cong\mathbb{E}(\Omega^{i+1}C,A)
for
i\geq 1
and
A,C\in\mathscr{C}
(see
[
LN19
, Sec. 5]
).

Let
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
be a cotorsion pair in
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
(see
[
NP19
, Def. 4.1]
), and assume it is

hereditary
(see
[
Yu22
, Def. 2.7]
, also
[
LZ23
, Def. 2.7]
), i.e.
\mathbb{E}^{i}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})=0
for all
i\geq 1
.
Note that
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{U}^{\perp_{1}}=\Set{A\in\mathscr{C}}{\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{U},A)=0}
, and so
\mathcal{V}
is extension-closed, closed under direct summands and contains
\mathcal{I}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{V}
is closed under cones of
\mathfrak{s}
-inflations. Indeed, like the exact case, it is known from
[
LN19
, Prop. 5.2]
that,
for any
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation
A\to B\to C,

the sequence

\mathbb{E}(U,A)\to\mathbb{E}(U,B)\to\mathbb{E}(U,C)\to\mathbb{E}^{2}(U,A)\to\mathbb{E}^{2}(U,B)\to\mathbb{E}^{2}(U,C)\to\cdots

is exact for any
U\in\mathcal{U}
.
Thus if
A,B\in\mathcal{V}
, then
C\in\mathcal{V}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{V}
fulfills all properties that we demand of
\mathscr{N}
in Setup
7.1
.

Since
\mathcal{V}
is extension-closed, it inherits an extriangulated structure from
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.
Thus, applying Proposition
7.2
to
\mathscr{N}=\mathcal{V}
measures the difference of these Grothendieck groups of
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathcal{V}
in some sense, namely, there is a right exact sequence
K_{0}(\mathcal{V})\to K_{0}(\mathscr{C})\to K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})\to 0
.

To show Proposition
7.2
, we first prepare a more detailed description of the factorization
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
w\colon A\to B
be a weak equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
Then there is a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
w\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{B}\end{pmatrix}}

of
w
, where

A\overset{\gamma_{A}}{\longrightarrow}I_{A}
is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation with
I_{A}\in\mathcal{I}
,

{\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
w\end{pmatrix}}\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}

and

{\begin{pmatrix}0,&\mathsf{id}_{B}\end{pmatrix}}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
.

Proof.

By the definition of
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
, the weak equivalence
w
admits a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

with
l\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
and
r\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
.
It follows that we may assume

C=I\oplus B
for some
I\in\mathcal{I}
,

r=\begin{pmatrix}0,&\mathsf{id}_{B}\end{pmatrix}
,
and

l=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma\\
w\end{pmatrix}\colon A\to I\oplus B
.

There is an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

A\overset{\gamma^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}I^{\prime}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma A

with
I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
as
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
has enough
\mathbb{E}
-injectives.
By Lemma
4.2
, there is a morphism

(7.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma\\
w\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(wPO)}}

of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles, where
N\in\mathscr{N}
,
as well as an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

(7.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}\oplus I\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{\prime}\\
\gamma\\
w\end{pmatrix}}

In addition, the bottom row of (
7.3
) splits as
I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
, and so
N^{\prime}\cong I^{\prime}\oplus N\in\mathscr{N}
.

Lastly, by
[
NP19
, Cor. 3.16]
, we have that

\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{\prime}\\
\gamma\end{pmatrix}\colon A\to I^{\prime}\oplus I

is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation. So we are done by setting

I_{A}\coloneqq I^{\prime}\oplus I
and

\gamma_{A}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{\prime}\\
\gamma\end{pmatrix}
.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

If
w\colon A\to B
is in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
and
\alpha\colon A\to J
is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation with
J\in\mathcal{I}
, then
\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

Proof.

Suppose we have the factorization of
w
as given in the statement of Lemma
7.4
,
and by assumption we can consider an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation
A\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}J\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma A.

We can construct
\mathfrak{s}
-conflations witnessing that

\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}

is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation in the following two ways.
First, start with an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
w\end{pmatrix}}

with
N^{\prime}\in\mathscr{N}

(see (
7.4
)), and
apply Lemma
4.2
to obtain
an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus J\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\oplus N^{\prime}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}}

Second, start with
A\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}J\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma A

and apply Lemma
4.2
twice to obtain
diagrams

(7.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(wPO)}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(wPO)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

the second of which gives rise to the
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus J\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus M.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}}

It follows that
I_{A}\oplus M\cong J\oplus N^{\prime}\in\mathscr{N}
, so
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\
w\end{pmatrix}=M\in\mathscr{N}
as
\mathscr{N}
is closed under direct summands.
∎

Lemma
7.4
also yields a characterization of the
\mathfrak{s}
-inflations in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
A\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}B\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}C\overset{\delta}{\dashrightarrow}
be any
\mathfrak{s}
-triangle.
Then
f\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
if and only if
f\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

Proof.

The forwards direction is clear. Thus suppose
f\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.
By Lemma
7.4
,
there exists an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation
\gamma_{A}\colon A\to I_{A}
with
I_{A}\in\mathcal{I}
such that the morphism
\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}\colon A\to I_{A}\oplus B
belongs to
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
By Lemma
4.2
, there is a morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(wPO)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles, in which the bottom row splits as
I_{A}\in\mathcal{I}
.
In particular,
C
is a direct summand of
N=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cone}}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}\in\mathscr{N}
, so
C\in\mathscr{N}
and we are done.
∎

The next lemma is just an easy application of the Horseshoe Lemma in the exact category case.
However, our category
\mathscr{C}
is extriangulated and we need a careful discussion.

Lemma 7.7
.

(cf.
[
Yu22
, Lem. 3.4]
)
Let
A_{1}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}A_{2}\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}A_{3}\overset{\delta}{\dashrightarrow}
be an
\mathfrak{s}
-triangle.
Then there exist
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles
A_{i}\overset{f_{i}}{\longrightarrow}I_{i}\overset{g_{i}}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma A_{i}\overset{\xi_{i}}{\dashrightarrow}
with
I_{i}\in\mathcal{I}
, and also a commutative diagram

(7.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{3}}

in which

(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}),(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}),(f,p,\Sigma f),(g,q,\Sigma g)
are morphisms of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles.

Proof.

First, for
i=1,3
consider
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles
A_{i}\overset{f_{i}}{\longrightarrow}I_{i}\overset{g_{i}}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma A_{i}\overset{\xi_{i}}{\dashrightarrow}
with
I_{i}\in\mathcal{I}
.
By
[
NP19
, Prop. 3.15]
and using that
I_{1}
is an
\mathbb{E}
-injective, we get the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{1}\oplus A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}a\\
g\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{id}_{I_{1}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{A_{3}}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{1}}

In particular, the morphism

\begin{pmatrix}a\\
g\end{pmatrix}\colon A_{2}\to I_{1}\oplus A_{3}

is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation.
The morphism

\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{id}_{I_{1}}\amph 0\\
0\amph f_{3}\end{pmatrix}

is a direct sum of
\mathfrak{s}
-inflations, so also an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation.
Thus, the composite

f_{2}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{id}_{I_{1}}\amph 0\\
0\amph f_{3}\end{pmatrix}\circ\begin{pmatrix}a\\
g\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a\\
f_{3}g\end{pmatrix}\colon A_{2}\to I_{1}\oplus I_{3}\eqqcolon I_{2}

is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation by
(ET4)
, and there is an
\mathfrak{s}
-triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{2}}

for some object
\Sigma A_{2}
.

Since
\begin{pmatrix}0,&\mathsf{id}_{I_{3}}\end{pmatrix}f_{2}=f_{3}g
,
the dual of
[
NP19
, Lem. 5.9]
yields
a commutative diagram

(7.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{id}_{I_{1}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,&\mathsf{id}_{I_{3}}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circlearrowleft}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{3}}

of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles and morphisms of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles.
On inspection, we see that
f^{\prime}_{1}=f_{1}
so
\xi^{\prime}_{1}\cong\xi_{1}
, and hence we may replace
\xi^{\prime}_{1}
by
\xi_{1}
to obtain (
7.6
).
∎

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
7.2
.

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

We already know
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
is a weak (bi)Waldhausen category.
To show
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
is a weak Waldhausen category, we need only check
(1)(WW0)
and
(1)(WW1)
.

For
(1)(WW0)
, it is clear that
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
contains
\operatorname{\mathsf{Iso}}\mathscr{C}
, so we just need to check
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
is closed under composition. First we note that
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
are each closed under composition as
\mathscr{N}
and
\mathcal{I}
, respectively, are extension-closed.
Each composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{B}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

in
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\circ\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
,
where
I\in\mathcal{I}
and
l\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
,
is also the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\oplus C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{I}\oplus l}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{C}\end{pmatrix}}

where

\mathsf{id}_{I}\oplus l

belongs to
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
as it is a direct sum of morphisms of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
,
and

\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{C}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\circ\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
, hence

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}\circ\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\circ\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
and

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
is closed under composition.

Now we will check
(WW1’)
(see Lemma
2.18
). Consider the following solid diagram of
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles in which
f^{\prime}a=bf
and where
a,b\in\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

(7.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}

Note that

does not require the triplet

First, by axiom (ET3) for an extriangulated category, we may complete the pair

Applying Lemma

to

we have a morphism

of

Therefore, we are reduced to showing

In particular,

.

, there is a commutative diagram

Consider the morphism

b_{3}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{\prime}\\
c_{1}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{B_{1}}f_{1}\\
f^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}-\mathsf{id}_{I_{B_{1}}}\amph\alpha\\
0\amph g^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{C^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}^{*}\delta^{\prime}}

for some automorphism
\beta
of
I_{B_{1}}\oplus C^{\prime}
.
Lastly, set

c^{\prime}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}0,\amph\mathsf{id}_{C^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\beta\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{\prime}\\
c_{1}\end{pmatrix}\colon C\to C^{\prime}
, which lies in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
as
I_{B_{1}}\in\mathcal{I}
.
One can then confirm the commutativity of (
7.8
) as required, and so
(WW1’)
holds for
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
.

We have thus checked that
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})
is a weak Waldhausen category
and hence established the sequence (
7.1
) lies in
\mathsf{wWald}
.
Now we check
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
. Recall that
\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
consists of the objects
C\in\mathscr{C}
for which
0\to C
lies in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.
As
\mathscr{C}
is weakly idempotent complete, the section

0\to N
belongs to
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
for all
N\in\mathscr{N}
by e.g.
[
BHST22
, Prop. 2.5]
,
so
\mathscr{N}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
.
Conversely, suppose we are given an object
X\in\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}
. Then, by the definition of
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
, we get morphisms
0\overset{l}{\to}N\overset{r}{\to}X
with
l\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
and
r\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}
.
In particular,
N\in\mathscr{N}
and
X
is a direct summand of
N
, which shows
X\in\mathscr{N}
.

To produce the right exact sequence (
7.2
), we will use Theorem
3.6
. First, note that Setup
3.1
is satisfied, so we may consider the classes defined in Definition
3.2
.
Since
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
,
it suffices to show
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{ac}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{C}}\cap\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}

and

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\subseteq\mathcal{T}=\set{g\in\operatorname{\mathsf{F}}\cap\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}{\text{there is an }\mathfrak{s}\text{-conflation }A\overset{f}{\to}B\overset{g}{\to}C\text{ with }A\in\mathscr{C}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}}=\mathscr{N}}.

Since
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
is precisely the class of
\mathfrak{s}
-inflations in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
by Corollary
7.6
,
the containment
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{ac}}
holds.
Since a morphism in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
is a retraction with cocone in
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathscr{N}
, we also see
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\subseteq\mathcal{T}
.
Therefore, by Theorem
3.6
we know (
7.1
) is a localization sequence in
\mathsf{wWald}
and
(
3.2
)
is right exact.
Finally, the weak Waldhausen categories
(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
and
(\mathscr{N},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})
are the canonical wbiWET structures on
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{N}
, respectively, arising from the extriangulated structure
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.
Hence, the isomorphisms
K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})\cong K_{0}(\mathscr{N},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}})
and
K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})\cong K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})
(see Example
5.1
) imply
the sequence (
7.2
) of Grothendieck groups is right exact, as claimed.
∎

7.2.
A connection to extriangulated localization

To clarify how the localization sequence (
7.1
) relates to extriangulated localization, we investigate the situation when
\mathscr{N}
is thick in
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.
We will see that, under Setup
7.8
below, our way of considering an extriangulated localization as a localization sequence of wWET categories as in Section
5
is equivalent to following the construction motivated by
[
Sar20
]
in Section
7.1
above.
Assume the following in the rest.

Setup 7.8
.

In addition to Setup
7.1
, we assume

\mathscr{N}
is thick in
(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
,

\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
is saturated and
\overline{\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}}
satisfies conditions
(MR1)–(MR4) of
[
NOS22
, p. 343]
.
Thus, there is the following extriangulated localization sequence (i.e. exact sequence) in
\mathsf{Extri}
(see Theorem
4.14
).

(7.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N},\mathbb{E}|_{\mathscr{N}},\mathfrak{s}|_{\mathscr{N}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N},\widetilde{\mathbb{E}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{inc},\iota)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Q,\mu)}

Thanks to Proposition
5.11
,
the exact sequence (
7.14
) in
\mathsf{Extri}
induces the following localization sequence in
\mathsf{wWald}
where
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
.

(7.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{N},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{V}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{inc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathscr{C}}}

Proposition 7.9
.

The localization sequences (
7.1
) and (
7.15
) coincide.
In particular, we have a group isomorphism

K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})=K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N},\widetilde{\mathbb{E}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.

Proof.

We have to only show that
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
coincides with
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
We immediately see that
\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
, so

\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
being the closure of
\mathcal{L}\cup\mathcal{R}
with respect to the composition (see Definition
4.13
) implies
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.
To show the converse, take a morphism
A\overset{f}{\to}B
in
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathscr{C}
has enough
\mathbb{E}
-injectives, there is an
\mathfrak{s}
-inflation
A\overset{\gamma_{A}}{\longrightarrow}I_{A}
with
I_{A}\in\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathscr{N}
which induces an
\mathfrak{s}
-conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{A}\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}}

Since
Q\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}\cong Qf
is an isomorphism in
\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N}
,
the morphism
\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}
belongs to
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}=\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
as
\mathscr{S}_{\mathscr{N}}
is saturated.
Thus, we have checked
N\in\operatorname{Ker}Q=\mathscr{N}
and
\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}
.
As

f=\begin{pmatrix}0,&\mathsf{id}_{B}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{A}\\
f\end{pmatrix}
, we have thus verified that
\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{I}}\circ\mathcal{K}_{\mathscr{N}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}
.

Therefore, we have

(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}})=(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})
.
Finally, from the proof of Corollary
5.14
we know that there is the group isomorphism

K_{0}(Q)\colon K_{0}(\mathscr{C},\operatorname{\mathsf{Seq}},\operatorname{\mathsf{W}}^{\prime})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}K_{0}(\mathscr{C}/\mathscr{N},\widetilde{\mathbb{E}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.
∎
